Alternatively use subgradient of max q with active weights μ_top. That may be more robust for min max. We can implement an iterative "maximum coefficient balancing" algorithm:
- Choose μ_j ∝ exp(β(q_j - maxq)) or top-K uniformly.
- Compute r_i = ∑ μ_j p_{j-i}.
- Update p_i ← p_i * exp(-η r_i) (since high r means i participates in high coefficients).
- Normalize.
This is like multiplicative gradient for max q with weights. Choose η and β. This is fast using FFT for r. We can do many iterations. Use exact evaluator to keep best. It might find good sequences.

Let's understand LP alternation. Given current p (sum=1), M=max q (exact). Solve LP: maximize ∑g_i subject to g_i≥0, and for all t, ∑_i p_i g_{t-i} ≤ M (i valid). This is a linear program. Let g be normalized to sum1: g_norm. Then consider f = (1-α)p + α g_norm. If f has lower max q? The SOTA proof maybe. We can implement for n modest with scipy linprog. This may strongly improve from uniform because LP will find g with low p*g; if p is near uniform, g maybe not uniform but some extreme. Updating may produce asymmetric p. Let's analyze uniform p=1/n with M=1/n. Constraint p*g_t = (1/n) number of g_i in window of length depending t ≤1/n => count ≤1. But g_i continuous, not count. Constraint for t=0: g_0≤1. t=1: g_0+g_1≤1, ..., t=n-1: ∑_i g_i ≤1, t=n: g_1+...+g_{n-1}≤1, etc. Max ∑g. Actually center constraint ∑g≤1, so max ∑g=1. Feasible any simplex with prefix/suffix constraints? For t small, g_0+...+g_t≤1, and later suffix; but total≤1 automatically if nonnegative. Edges constraints maybe not binding if total≤1? For t=1, g0+g1≤1, total≤1 implies. So LP feasible set total≤1, any distribution? Wait center t=n-1 has ∑_{i=0}^{n-1} p_i g_{n-1-i}= (1/n)∑ g =∑g/n ≤1/n -> ∑g≤1. Edge t=0: (1/n)g0≤1/n -> g0≤1, automatic if total1. So any g with sum≤1 feasible. LP will have unbounded? Objective max sum, center gives sum≤1, so max1, many optima. Need choose extreme maybe via objective tie-break? linprog may return a vertex with total1, but constraints not enough? There are 2n-1 constraints; total1 and many prefix constraints. The polytope {g≥0, ∑prefix? total≤1} just simplex; LP max sum will return any feasible sum=1, e.g. all mass at a variable? If linprog chooses g0=1, then update f=(1-α)/n + (1-1/n) e0? This has huge p_0, bad. Need tie-break to choose good g. We can add small secondary objective to prefer smooth g or negative correlation? The LP as stated may not progress if degenerate. SOTA maybe solves with f not exactly uniform? But uniform stuck maybe explains previous 2.0.

def cross_corr_mu_p(mu,p):
  # Need r_i = ∑_k p_k mu_{i+k}, i=0..n-1.
  # We can use convolution of p with reversed mu: c = conv(p, mu[::-1])

Implementation of smooth optimizer:
```
def softmax(z):
  z = z - np.max(z)
  # clip for stability
  z = np.clip(z, -50, 50)
  e=np.exp(z); s=e.sum(); return e/s
```
But clipping z in objective makes gradient zero outside; maybe okay. To avoid variable order effects, z centered (subtract mean) in softmax? Since adding constant to z no effect, L-BFGS may drift; we can periodically recenter or add tiny penalty on mean? In gradient, grad_z sum0. We can subtract gradient mean to keep.

```
def lse_gradient(z,beta):
 p=softmax(z); q=conv; u=beta*(q-qmax); mu=exp(u)/sum; grad_p=2*beta*r where r=cross_corr(mu,p)
 grad_z=p*(grad_p - p@grad_p)
```

1. Define helper `score_list(seq)`: clamp and evaluate_sequence. Also `score_p(p)`: normalize and evaluate; avoid re-list for internal?
```
def internal_score(p):
  p=np.asarray(p,float); p=np.clip(p,0,1000); n=p.size; s=p.sum(); if s<0.01 return inf; q=np.convolve(p,p); return 2.0*n*q.max()/ (s*s)
```
For sequences with very large values, q may overflow? Values ≤1000, n maybe 10000, sum 1e7, q 1e14, float ok. We can normalize to sum1 for internal score to avoid overflow:
```
if s>0: p=p/s; q=np.convolve(p,p); return 2*n*q.max()
```
Because scale invariant. This avoids sum**2. Use `np.convolve` direct for n≤2000? For large n, direct O(n²) may be slow. But we can choose n limited. If using height_sequence_1 length6962, direct convolve 48M per score, maybe okay for a few but not many. For large refinement, use FFT? But internal score could use `scipy.signal.fftconvolve` if available, else direct. Simpler: if n>1000, use fftconvolve.

Implement FFT convolution for nonnegative real:
```
def fast_convolve(a,b):
  size=len(a)+len(b)-1; N=1; while N<size: N*=2? Use next power of two by bit_length.
  A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); B=np.fft.rfft(b,N); return np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)[:size]
```
For same sequence. Negative tiny due to float set to0.
Use for q if n>300 maybe.

Gradient correlation `corr_mu_p` similarly for n>300 use `fftconvolve(p, mu[::-1])` slice. But if n small use np.correlate direct.

2. L-BFGS optimizer:
```
def lbfgs_refine(p, n, deadline, beta=50, maxiter=200, zmax=8):
  z=np.log(p+1e-300); z-=z.mean()
  def fun(): ...
  res=minimize(fun, z, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=[(-zmax,zmax)]*n, options={...})
  if res.success or res.x: p=softmax(res.x); threshold small; return p
```
Need `fun` as top-level helper? Python nested functions are closures; rules: "Don't use any lambda functions. Make all helper functions top level and have no closures from function nesting." So cannot define nested fun capturing beta. We need top-level objective function that takes args and maybe a global/state? But no closures. We can implement a class? Rules say helper functions top-level and no closures from function nesting. Classes with methods okay? It says no closures, not no classes. But safer to avoid nested functions; use top-level function that receives parameters through a global variable? We can set module-level `_LBFGS_STATE` dictionary before calling minimize, and objective reads it. But that's a global mutable, allowed. Example:
```
_LBFGS_STATE = {}
def _lbfgs_fun(z):
   p=softmax(z)
   q=convolve(p,p)
   beta=_LBFGS_STATE['beta']; n=_LBFGS_STATE['n']
   ...
   return val, grad
```
Then in `lbfgs_refine`, set `_LBFGS_STATE = {...}`? If helper top-level, modifying global okay. But `scipy.optimize.minimize` expects function no extra args. This works, no nested closure. Need ensure no lambda.

But state global may be overwritten by concurrent? Only 1 CPU sequential.

3. Multiplicative optimizer top-level:
```
def mirror_refine(p, n, deadline, beta0=10.0, step_rad=0.5, top_fraction=1.0):
  p=p/sum; z=np.log(p+1e-300); z-=z.mean(); best_p=p.copy(); best_score=internal_score(p)
  beta=beta0
  decay=1.0
  while time<deadline:
    q=convolve(p,p); M=q.max(); u=beta*(q-M); expu=exp(clip u); mu=expu/expu.sum()
    r=corr_mu_p(mu,p)
    centered=r-r.mean(); scale=max(abs(centered).max(),1e-12)
    # try a few steps, keep best
    improved=False
    for frac in [2.0,1.0,0.5,0.2,0.05,0.01]:
       z_new=z - step_rad*frac*(centered/scale) # or maybe add? 
       p_new=softmax(z_new)
       sc=internal_score(p_new)
       if sc<best_score: best...
    If no improvement, adapt beta = min(beta*1.5, 200*n?), maybe reset z random? 
```
This tries exact objective not just LSE. Could be slower (6 evals per iter) but okay for n moderate. For large n, reduce tries.

The sign: centered high r -> z decreases (subtract). If centered negative -> z increases. Good.

But softmax with z centered each time loses absolute scale? Since p invariant to mean, subtract mean okay. To prevent z from all going to zero (uniform), we need maintain variance? Subtracting mean of centered update doesn't collapse because centered has mean0, z mean stays. But if no improvement, maybe reset.

A more natural multiplicative update from LSE: z_new = z - learning_rate * grad_z? grad_z magnitude O(β) p etc. But r scaling easier.

Maybe use direct `p_new = p / (1 + η max(centered,0))`? Not.

Given time, L-BFGS is better. Mirror fallback.

Implement `softmax` with stable and no overflow:
```
def softmax(z):
  z=np.asarray(z,float)
  z=z-z.max()
  z=np.clip(z,-50,50)
  e=np.exp(z)
  s=e.sum()
  if not finite or s<=0: return np.full_like(z,1/z.size)
  return e/s
```
Clipping to 50 after centering; if all z shifted, okay.

`fast_conv_same(a)`:
```
def convolution_same(a):
  n=a.size
  if n<300:
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
  else:
    size=2*n-1
    N=1
    while N<size: N<<=1
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:size]
    c=np.maximum(c,0) # small negatives
  return c
```
If a contains zeros, fine. Direct np.convolve returns float. For large values normalized sum1, q ~1/n, no overflow.

`corr_mu_p(mu,p)`:
```
def cross_mu_p(mu,p):
  n=p.size
  if n<512:
    c=np.correlate(mu,p,mode='full')
    return c[n-1:n+n-1] # n terms? start n-1 to start+n
  # For larger, use fftconvolve p with mu_rev; start = len(mu)-1; slice descending?
```
Check slice for `np.correlate` start n-1 length n gives positive k 0..n-1, which we proved. For n=2, `c[1:3]` length2. Good. `n+n-1` end exclusive = 2n-1; full length m+n-1=3n-2, for n=2 length4, end3 gives indexes1,2. Good.

For n large, FFT maybe:
```
size = len(mu)+n-1; N next pow2; P=rfft(p,N); U=rfft(mu[::-1],N); c=irfft(P*U,N)[:size]; start=len(mu)-1; idx=start-np.arange(n); return c[idx]
```
Ensure idx valid: start=2n-2, min idx=n-1. c length 3n-3. Good.

But `np.correlate` direct for n=512 m=1023 => ~500k per call, okay for L-BFGS? maybe. Use threshold 256 for FFT.

`lbfgs_state` objective:
```
_OBJ = {'beta':1.0,'n':1}
def _obj(z):
  n=_OBJ['n']; beta=_OBJ['beta']; zmax=_OBJ['zmax']
  z=np.clip(z, -zmax, zmax)  # does not affect if bounds used
  p=softmax(z); p=p/p.sum()
  q=convolution_same(p)
  qmax=float(np.max(q))
  u=beta*(q-qmax)
  e=np.exp(np.clip(u,-50,50))
  s=e.sum()
  mu=e/s
  r=cross_mu_p(mu,p)
  grad_p=2.0*beta*r
  mean_grad=float(np.dot(p,grad_p))
  grad_z=p*(grad_p-mean_grad)
  # optional entropy regularizer? Could include small `reg=1e-4*n*var(z)`? Maybe not.
  val=beta*qmax + math.log(float(s))
  # To discourage extreme z, add 1e-4 * sum(z^2) after centering?
  zc=z-z.mean(); val += 1e-5 * float(np.dot(zc,zc))
  grad_z += 2e-5*(zc)  # derivative
  return val, grad_z
```
Adding regularization helps stability. The coefficient small; might slightly bias uniform but okay. For target, don't want bias too strong. Use 1e-6 maybe. If n large, var ~O(1), okay. We can set reg=1e-4? Let's use 1e-5. It changes objective but final exact eval authoritative. It can prevent L-BFGS from wandering to extreme z.

But if optimum has zeros (support smaller), reg against large z may prevent reaching zero. zmax 8 allows zeros approximate e^-16/sum ~1e-7, enough.

```
best_p=p.copy(); best_score=internal_score(p)
for beta in betas:
  if time > deadline: break
  _OBJ.update(beta=beta,n=n,zmax=zmax)
  maxiter = 80 if n<=16 else 40 if n<=64 else 20
  before=time
  res=minimize(_obj, z, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=[(-zmax,zmax)]*n, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-7,'maxls':20})
  if res is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)): z=res.x
  else: maybe break
  p=softmax(z); p=np.clip(p,0,None); p/=sum; threshold: p[p<1e-10]=0; renorm
  sc=internal_score(p); update
  if time>deadline: break
return best_p,best_score
```

```
def gradient_local(p, n, deadline):
  z=np.log(p+1e-300); z-=z.mean()
  best=p; best_sc=internal_score(p)
  step=0.1
  while time<deadline:
    q=conv; M=q.max()
    # active mu top K uniformly e.g. K=max(1,int(0.1*m))
    order=np.argsort(q)[::-1]; K=max(1,min(m,len(order)//10)); mu=np.zeros(m); mu[order[:K]]=1/K
    r=cross(mu,p); centered=r-mean
    scale=max(abs(centered).max(),1e-12)
    # Try multiple steps with exact score
    improved=False
    for f in [3,1,0.3,0.1,0.03]:
      z_new= z - f*centered/scale
      p_new=softmax(z_new); p_new[np.abs(p_new)<1e-12]=0; normalize
      sc=internal
      if sc<best_sc: update z/p/best; improved=True; break
    if not improved:
      step? random z perturbation: z += rng.normal(0,0.2); z-=mean; maybe beta change
  return best
```
This is similar mirror but exact line search. Could use after L-BFGS for best n.

Detailed `lbfgs_refine`:
```
def lbfgs_refine(p, n, deadline, per_beta_iters=None):
    zmax=8.0 if n<=32 else 6.0
    z=np.log(np.asarray(p,float)+1e-300)
    z=z-np.mean(z)
    z=np.clip(z,-zmax,zmax)
    best_p=np.array(p,float); best_p/=best_p.sum()
    best_score=internal_score(best_p)
    if not _HAVE_SCIPY:
        return best_p,best_score
    if n <= 64:
        if n <= 16:
            betas=(4.0,16.0,64.0,256.0,1000.0)
        elif n <= 32:
            betas=(8.0,32.0,128.0,512.0)
        else:
            betas=(16.0,64.0,256.0)
    else:
        b1=max(10.0, float(n))
        betas=(b1, min(5*b1, 20000.0), min(20*b1, 50000.0))
    reg=1e-5 if n<=64 else 1e-6
    bounds=[(-zmax,zmax)]*n
    for beta in betas:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
        _OBJ_STATE['beta']=float(beta); _OBJ_STATE['n']=int(n); _OBJ_STATE['zmax']=zmax; _OBJ_STATE['reg']=reg
        maxiter = 70 if n<=12 else 40 if n<=32 else 20
        try:
            res=_scipy_minimize(_obj, z, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-7, 'maxls':20})
        except Exception:
            res=None
        if res is not None and hasattr(res,'x') and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
            z=np.asarray(res.x,float)
        # z may drift; clip and center
        z=np.clip(z,-zmax,zmax); z=z-np.mean(z)
        p2=softmax(z)
        p2=np.clip(p2,0.0,None)
        p2=p2[p2<1e-11]=0 (careful boolean indexing assignment)
        ss=p2.sum()
        if ss<=0: continue
        p2=p2/ss
        sc=internal_score(p2)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_p=p2.copy(); best_score=float(sc)
        # if no improvement for this beta maybe chance of local? no.
    return best_p,best_score
```
In Python, `p2[p2<1e-11]=0` valid. Good.

But `internal_score` uses `scipy`? no.

`mirror_refine` implementation:
```
def mirror_refine(p,n,deadline):
  z=...; best...; beta = max(1.0, 5.0*n if n>32 else 8.0)
  step=0.5
  no_improve=0
  while time<deadline:
    q=convolution_same(p); M=maxq; u=beta*(q-M); e=exp(clip(u,-50,50)); mu=e/sum
    r=cross_mu_p(mu,p); centered=r-np.mean(r); scale=max(abs(centered).max(),1e-12)
    improved=False
    for f in (2.0,1.0,0.5,0.2,0.05,0.01):
       z_new=z - f*step*(centered/scale)
       z_new=np.clip(z_new,-8,8); z_new-=mean
       p2=softmax(z_new); zero threshold; normalize
       sc=internal_score(p2)
       if sc<best_score: update; improved=True; break
    if improved: no_improve=0; step*=1.05
    else:
       no_improve+=1
       if no_improve>5:
          # perturb z randomly or change beta
          beta *= 1.5; if beta>1e6: beta=n*50
```

```
  elif4: x1=rng.random(n)*n; s=rng.uniform(0.05,0.3)*n; p=np.exp(-((i-x1)**2)/(2*s*s))+np.exp(-((i-n+1-x1)**2)/(2*s*s))? maybe too.

  return p normalized
```

Let's implement `local_top_gradient` robust:
```
def local_top_gradient(p, n, deadline, rng, max_steps_no_improve=20):
  z=np.log(p+1e-300); z -= mean; best=p; best_sc=internal_score(p)
  beta=20.0
  no=0
  while time<deadline:
    q=convolution_same(p); M=max; m=len(q)
    # active weights: logistic or top
    if rng.random()<0.5:
       u=beta*(q-M); e=exp(clip(u,-50,50)); mu=e/sum
    else:
       order=np.argsort(q)[::-1]; K=max(1,m//5); mu=zeros; mu[order[:K]]=1/K
    r=cross_mu_p(mu,p); centered=r-mean; scale=max(abs(centered).max(),1e-12)
    improved=False
    for f in (2.0,1.0,0.5,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01):
       z_new=z - f*(centered/scale)
       z_new=clip(z_new,-8,8); z_new-=mean
       p2=softmax(z_new); p2[p2<1e-11]=0; normalize
       sc=internal_score(p2)
       if sc<best_sc: update; improved=True; break
    if improved: no=0; beta=min(beta*1.1, 1000)
    else:
       no+=1
       if no>8:
          beta = beta*2 if beta<1000 else 10
          z += 0.25*rng.normal(size=n); z-=mean
          no=0
  return best
```
This uses exact score many times; for n small, fine. For large n, exact score expensive, but call only if size <=256 perhaps.

```
import time
```

`softmax` uses `np.nan_to_num`? If z array contains inf due L-BFGS? `z=np.asarray(z,dtype=float); z=np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=50, neginf=-50); z=max clip`.

```
def softmax(z):
    z = np.nan_to_num(np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64), nan=0.0, posinf=50.0, neginf=-50.0)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 50.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(z.shape, 1.0 / max(1, z.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s
```

`convolution_same`:
```
def convolution_same(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n==1: return np.array([a[0]*a[0]])
    if n <= 350:
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
       c=np.asarray(c,float)
    else:
       size=2*n-1; N=1
       while N < size: N <<= 1
       fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:size]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```
Using direct for n<=350; for 350, 122k ops? n^2=122k actually 350^2=122k, good. 1000 ->1M, fft maybe faster. Use threshold 500 maybe. Fine.

`cross_mu_p`:
```
def cross_mu_p(mu,p):
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 350:
       c=np.correlate(mu,p,mode='full')
       start=n-1
       return np.asarray(c[start:start+n],float)
    size=n+len(mu)-1; N=1; while N<size: N<<=1
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N); fm=np.fft.rfft(mu[::-1],N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fm,N)[:size]
    start=len(mu)-1
    return c[start - np.arange(n)]
```
Need ensure c not negative? mu,p nonneg, c nonneg, but FFT tiny neg; could `np.maximum(...,0)`. Good.

`internal_score` as above. Maybe if `n` large and `p` sum=1, `qmax` ~? Use math.isfinite.

`_obj`: Use `global OBJ`? We modify dict entries, no global keyword needed.
```
def _obj(z):
    beta=float(OBJ['beta']); n=int(OBJ['n']); zmax=float(OBJ['zmax']); reg=float(OBJ['reg'])
    z=np.nan_to_num(np.asarray(z,float), nan=0.0, posinf=zmax, neginf=-zmax)
    z=np.clip(z,-zmax,zmax)
    p=softmax(z)
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    ss=p.sum()
    if ss <=0: return 1e100, np.zeros_like(z)
    p=p/ss
    q=convolution_same(p)
    qmax=float(np.max(q))
    u=beta*(q-qmax)
    e=np.exp(np.clip(u,-50,50))
    s=float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s<=0:
       return 1e100, np.zeros_like(z)
    mu=e/s
    r=cross_mu_p(mu,p)
    grad_p=2.0*beta*r
    mg=float(np.dot(p,grad_p))
    grad_z=p*(grad_p-mg)
    zc=z - np.mean(z)
    val=beta*qmax + math.log(s) + reg*float(np.dot(zc,zc))
    grad_z=grad_z + 2.0*reg*zc
    if not math.isfinite(val):
       val=1e100
       grad_z=zeros
    return val, grad_z
```

```
def anneal_small(p,n,deadline,rng):
  z=log(p); best...
  T=0.5; alpha=0.999
  while time<deadline:
    z_new = z + rng.normal(0,sigma,n); center; maybe occasional zero set
    p_new=softmax; score
    if score<best: update; z=z_new; T*=...
    else if rng.random() < exp((best-score)/T) and score<best*1.5: z=z_new; T*=...
    sigma adjust
```

`make_start` code:
```
def make_start(n, rng, kind):
    n=int(n)
    if n <=0: return np.ones(1)
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    k=int(kind) % 7
    if k==0:
      p=np.ones(n) * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.1,n))
    elif k==1:
      alpha=float(rng.choice((0.1,0.3,0.7,2.0,10.0)))
      p=rng.dirichlet(alpha*np.ones(n))
    elif k==2:
      gam=float(rng.uniform(-4.0,4.0))
      if n>1:
         if gam>=0: p=np.exp(gam*i/(n-1))
         else: p=np.exp(-gam*(n-1-i)/(n-1))
      else: p=np.ones(1)
      p=p + 1e-3
    elif k==3:
      z=rng.normal(size=n)
      win=max(1, n//8)
      if n >= win:
        z=np.convolve(z, np.ones(win)/win, mode='same')
      p=softmax(z)
    elif k==4:
      x1=float(rng.uniform(0.25,0.75)*n)
      x2=float(rng.uniform(0.25,0.75)*n)
      s1=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.25)*n)
      s2=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.25)*n)
      p=np.exp(-((i-x1)**2)/(2*s1*s1)) + np.exp(-((i-x2)**2)/(2*s2*s2))
      p = p + 1e-3
    elif k==5:
      p=rng.random(size=n)
      mask=rng.random(size=n) < 0.35
      p[mask]=0.0
      p = p + 1e-3
    else:
      base=2
      kbits=0
      m=n
      while (1 << kbits) < n: kbits+=1
      if (1 << kbits) == n:
        probs=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(base, dtype=float))
        p=np.zeros(n)
        for idx in range(n):
          val=1.0; x=idx
          for _ in range(kbits):
            d=x & 1
            val *= probs[d]
            x >>=1
          p[idx]=val
      else:
        p=rng.random(n)
        p = np.sort(p)?? maybe no.
    p=np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s=float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s<=0.0:
       p=np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    else:
       p=p/s
    return p
```
For product measure with base2 and kbits=0 n=1? if n=1 (1<<0)=1, kbits=0, loop none p[0]=1. Good.

In `block_reduce`:
```
def block_reduce(a,target):
  a=np.asarray(a,float); n=a.size
  if target<=0 or n==0: return np.ones(target if target>0 else 1)
  if target >= n: return a.copy()
  out=np.zeros(target)
  bounds=np.linspace(0,n,target+1,dtype=int)
  for j in range(target):
    out[j]=np.sum(a[bounds[j]:bounds[j+1]])
  s=out.sum()
  if s<=0 or not finite: out=np.ones(target)
  else: out=out/s
  return out
```

```
def mirror_refine(p, n, deadline, rng):
  p=np.asarray(p,float).copy(); p=np.clip(p,0,1000)
  s=p.sum(); if s<=0: p=np.ones(n)
  p=p/s
  best_p=p.copy(); best_score=internal_score(best_p)
  z=np.log(p+1e-300); z-=mean; z=np.clip(z,-8,8)
  beta=max(8.0, 2.0*n if n>32 else 20.0)
  no=0; zmax=8.0
  while time.monotonic()<deadline:
    q=convolution_same(p); M=float(np.max(q)); u=beta*(q-M); e=np.exp(np.clip(u,-50,50)); s=e.sum(); if s<=0: break; mu=e/s
    r=cross_mu_p(mu,p); centered=r - float(np.mean(r)); scale=float(np.max(np.abs(centered)))
    if not finite or scale<=1e-15: break
    improved=False
    for frac in (2.0,1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05):
      z_new=z - frac*(centered/scale)
      z_new=np.clip(z_new,-zmax,zmax); z_new -= float(np.mean(z_new))
      p2=softmax(z_new); p2=np.maximum(p2,0); ss=p2.sum(); if ss<=0: continue
      p2=p2/ss
      sc=internal_score(p2)
      if finite and sc<best_score:
         best_p=p2.copy(); best_score=float(sc); z=z_new; p=p2.copy(); improved=True; break
    if improved:
       no=0; beta=min(beta*1.05, 20000)
    else:
       no +=1
       beta = beta*1.6 if beta<20000 else 10.0
       if no >= 8:
          z = z + 0.3*rng.normal(size=n)
          z -= mean; z=clip; p=softmax(z); p/=sum
          no=0
  return best_p, best_score
```

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a)^2).

The optimizer is a multi-length portfolio search:
  * many small lengths are explored with L-BFGS in log-softmax space,
    using a smoothed log-sum-exp objective;
  * fallback tempered mirror/local-search is used for larger lengths;
  * the best exact sequence found is returned.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize as scipy_minimize
    _HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SCIPY = False

# Shared state for the top-level L-BFGS callback.
_LBFGS_STATE = {
    "beta": 1.0,
    "n": 1,
    "zmax": 8.0,
    "reg": 1e-5,
}


def _softmax(z):
    z = np.nan_to_num(np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64),
                      nan=0.0, posinf=50.0, neginf=-50.0)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 50.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(z.shape, 1.0 / max(1, z.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def _convolution_same(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0] * a[0]], dtype=np.float64)

    if n <= 350:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
        c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        size = 2 * n - 1
        N = 1
        while N < size:
            N <<= 1
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:size]
        c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)

    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _cross_mu_p(mu, p):
    """Return r_i = sum_k p_k mu_{i+k}, i=0..n-1."""
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    mu = np.asarray(mu, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)

    if n <= 350:
        c = np.correlate(mu, p, mode="full")
        start = n - 1
        return np.asarray(c[start:start + n], dtype=np.float64)

    size = n + len(mu) - 1
    N = 1
    while N < size:
        N <<= 1
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    fm = np.fft.rfft(mu[::-1], N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fm, N)[:size]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    start = len(mu) - 1
    return np.asarray(c[start - np.arange(n)], dtype=np.float64)


def _internal_score(seq):
    """Fast evaluator-equivalent score for internally normalized sequences."""
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    n = a.size
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    a = a / s
    q = _convolution_same(a)
    if q.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    qmax = float(np.max(q))
    if not math.isfinite(qmax):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * qmax)


def _lbfgs_objective(z):
    beta = float(_LBFGS_STATE["beta"])
    n = int(_LBFGS_STATE["n"])
    zmax = float(_LBFGS_STATE["zmax"])
    reg = float(_LBFGS_STATE["reg"])

    z = np.nan_to_num(np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64),
                      nan=0.0, posinf=zmax, neginf=-zmax)
    z = np.clip(z, -zmax, zmax)

    p = _softmax(z)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    ss = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(ss) or ss <= 0.0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(z)
    p = p / ss

    q = _convolution_same(p)
    qmax = float(np.max(q))
    u = beta * (q - qmax)
    e = np.exp(np.clip(u, -50.0, 50.0))
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(z)

    mu = e / s
    r = _cross_mu_p(mu, p)

    grad_p = 2.0 * beta * r
    mean_grad = float(np.dot(p, grad_p))
    grad_z = p * (grad_p - mean_grad)

    zc = z - float(np.mean(z))
    val = beta * qmax + math.log(s) + reg * float(np.dot(zc, zc))
    grad_z = grad_z + 2.0 * reg * zc

    if not math.isfinite(val):
        val = 1e100
        grad_z = np.zeros_like(z)

    return float(val), grad_z


def _lbfgs_refine(p, deadline):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.ones(4, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        p = p / s

    n = int(p.size)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = _internal_score(best_p)

    if not _HAVE_SCIPY:
        return best_p, best_score

    zmax = 8.0 if n <= 32 else 6.0
    z = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    z -= float(np.mean(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -zmax, zmax)

    if n <= 16:
        betas = (4.0, 16.0, 64.0, 256.0, 1000.0)
        maxiter = 70
    elif n <= 32:
        betas = (8.0, 32.0, 128.0, 512.0)
        maxiter = 45
    else:
        b1 = max(10.0, float(n))
        betas = (b1, min(5.0 * b1, 20000.0), min(20.0 * b1, 50000.0))
        maxiter = 25

    reg = 1e-5 if n <= 64 else 1e-6
    bounds = [(-zmax, zmax)] * n

    for beta in betas:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        _LBFGS_STATE["beta"] = float(beta)
        _LBFGS_STATE["n"] = n
        _LBFGS_STATE["zmax"] = zmax
        _LBFGS_STATE["reg"] = reg

        try:
            res = scipy_minimize(
                _lbfgs_objective,
                z,
                jac=True,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                bounds=bounds,
                options={
                    "maxiter": maxiter,
                    "ftol": 1e-12,
                    "gtol": 1e-7,
                    "maxls": 20,
                },
            )
        except Exception:
            res = None

        if res is not None and hasattr(res, "x") and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
            z = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)

        z = np.clip(z, -zmax, zmax)
        z -= float(np.mean(z))

        p2 = _softmax(z)
        p2 = np.maximum(p2, 0.0)
        p2[p2 < 1e-11] = 0.0
        ss = float(p2.sum())
        if ss <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(ss):
            continue
        p2 = p2 / ss

        sc = _internal_score(p2)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_p = p2.copy()
            best_score = float(sc)

    return best_p, best_score


def _mirror_refine(p, deadline, rng):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        p = np.ones(4, dtype=np.float64)
        n = 4
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        p = p / s

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = _internal_score(best_p)

    z = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    z -= float(np.mean(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -8.0, 8.0)

    beta = max(8.0, 2.0 * float(n) if n > 32 else 20.0)
    zmax = 8.0
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        q = _convolution_same(p)
        qmax = float(np.max(q))
        u = beta * (q - qmax)
        e = np.exp(np.clip(u, -50.0, 50.0))
        ss = float(e.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(ss) or ss <= 0.0:
            break
        mu = e / ss

        r = _cross_mu_p(mu, p)
        centered = r - float(np.mean(r))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(centered)))
        if not math.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-15:
            z = z + 0.25 * rng.normal(size=n)
            z -= float(np.mean(z))
            p = _softmax(z)
            p = p / float(p.sum())
            continue

        improved = False
        for frac in (2.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.05):
            z_new = z - frac * (centered / scale)
            z_new = np.clip(z_new, -zmax, zmax)
            z_new -= float(np.mean(z_new))

            p2 = _softmax(z_new)
            p2 = np.maximum(p2, 0.0)
            p2[p2 < 1e-11] = 0.0
            ss2 = float(p2.sum())
            if not math.isfinite(ss2) or ss2 <= 0.0:
                continue
            p2 = p2 / ss2

            sc = _internal_score(p2)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_p = p2.copy()
                best_score = float(sc)
                z = z_new
                p = p2.copy()
                improved = True
                no_improve = 0
                beta = min(beta * 1.05, 20000.0)
                break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            beta = beta * 1.6 if beta < 20000.0 else 10.0
            if no_improve >= 8:
                z = z + 0.3 * rng.normal(size=n)
                z = np.clip(z, -zmax, zmax)
                z -= float(np.mean(z))
                p = _softmax(z)
                p = p / float(p.sum())
                no_improve = 0

    return best_p, best_score


def _local_top_gradient(p, deadline, rng):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n = int(p.size)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        p = p / s

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = _internal_score(best_p)

    z = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    z -= float(np.mean(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -8.0, 8.0)
    zmax = 8.0

    beta = 20.0
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        q = _convolution_same(p)
        qmax = float(np.max(q))
        m = q.size

        mode = int(rng.integers(0, 3))
        if mode == 0:
            u = beta * (q - qmax)
            e = np.exp(np.clip(u, -50.0, 50.0))
            ss = float(e.sum())
            if not math.isfinite(ss) or ss <= 0.0:
                mu = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / m
            else:
                mu = e / ss
        elif mode == 1:
            k = max(1, m // 5)
            order = np.argsort(q)[::-1][:k]
            mu = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
            mu[order] = 1.0 / k
        else:
            k = max(1, m // 12)
            order = np.argsort(q)[::-1][:k]
            mu = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
            mu[order] = 1.0 / k

        r = _cross_mu_p(mu, p)
        centered = r - float(np.mean(r))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(centered)))
        if not math.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-15:
            z = z + 0.25 * rng.normal(size=n)
            z = np.clip(z, -zmax, zmax)
            z -= float(np.mean(z))
            p = _softmax(z)
            p = p / float(p.sum())
            continue

        improved = False
        for frac in (2.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02):
            z_new = z - frac * (centered / scale)
            z_new = np.clip(z_new, -zmax, zmax)
            z_new -= float(np.mean(z_new))

            p2 = _softmax(z_new)
            p2 = np.maximum(p2, 0.0)
            p2[p2 < 1e-11] = 0.0
            ss2 = float(p2.sum())
            if not math.isfinite(ss2) or ss2 <= 0.0:
                continue
            p2 = p2 / ss2

            sc = _internal_score(p2)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_p = p2.copy()
                best_score = float(sc)
                z = z_new
                p = p2.copy()
                improved = True
                no_improve = 0
                beta = min(beta * 1.1, 2000.0)
                break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            beta = beta * 1.8 if beta < 4000.0 else 15.0
            if no_improve >= 8:
                z = z + 0.35 * rng.normal(size=n)
                z = np.clip(z, -zmax, zmax)
                z -= float(np.mean(z))
                p = _softmax(z)
                p = p / float(p.sum())
                no_improve = 0

    return best_p, best_score


def _make_start(n, rng, kind):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    k = int(kind) % 7

    if k == 0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.10, n))
    elif k == 1:
        alpha = float(rng.choice((0.1, 0.3, 0.7, 2.0, 8.0)))
        p = rng.dirichlet(alpha * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    elif k == 2:
        gam = float(rng.uniform(-4.0, 4.0))
        if n > 1:
            if gam >= 0.0:
                p = np.exp(gam * i / float(n - 1))
            else:
                p = np.exp((-gam) * (n - 1 - i) / float(n - 1))
        else:
            p = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
        p = p + 1e-3
    elif k == 3:
        z = rng.normal(size=n)
        win = max(1, n // 8)
        if n >= win:
            z = np.convolve(z, np.ones(win, dtype=np.float64) / float(win), mode="same")
        p = _softmax(z)
    elif k == 4:
        x1 = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 0.75)) * float(n)
        x2 = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 0.75)) * float(n)
        s1 = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.25)) * float(n)
        s2 = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.25)) * float(n)
        p = np.exp(-((i - x1) ** 2) / (2.0 * s1 * s1))
        p += np.exp(-((i - x2) ** 2) / (2.0 * s2 * s2))
        p = p + 1e-3
    elif k == 5:
        p = rng.random(size=n).astype(np.float64)
        mask = rng.random(size=n) < 0.35
        p[mask] = 0.0
        p = p + 1e-3
    else:
        # Product binary measure when n is a power of two; otherwise simple random.
        base = 2
        kbits = 0
        while (1 << kbits) < n:
            kbits += 1
        if (1 << kbits) == n:
            probs = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(base, dtype=np.float64))
            p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            for idx in range(n):
                val = 1.0
                x = idx
                for _ in range(kbits):
                    d = x & 1
                    val *= float(probs[d])
                    x >>= 1
                p[idx] = val
        else:
            p = rng.random(size=n).astype(np.float64)
            p = p + 1e-3

    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        p = p / s
    return p


def _block_reduce(a, target):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    target = int(max(1, target))
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(target, dtype=np.float64)
    if target >= n:
        return a.copy()

    bounds = np.floor(np.linspace(0, n, target + 1)).astype(int)
    out = np.zeros(target, dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(target):
        out[j] = float(np.sum(a[bounds[j]:bounds[j + 1]]))

    s = float(out.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        out = np.ones(target, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        out = out / s
    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Search for the best sequence within the allotted wall-clock budget."""
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 8.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    best_p = np.ones(4, dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = _internal_score(best_p)

    # Use provided construction as a seed if available.
    h1 = None
    if "height_sequence_1" in globals():
        try:
            h1 = np.asarray(globals()["height_sequence_1"], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            h1 = np.nan_to_num(h1, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            h1 = np.clip(h1, 0.0, 1000.0)
        except Exception:
            h1 = None

    if h1 is not None and h1.size > 0:
        s = float(h1.sum())
        if math.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
            hh = h1 / s
            sc = _internal_score(hh)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = float(sc)
                best_p = hh.copy()
                print(f"initial height_sequence_1 score={best_score:.10f} len={h1.size}", flush=True)
        for target in (16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024):
            if h1.size > target and time.monotonic() < deadline:
                p = _block_reduce(h1, target)
                sc = _internal_score(p)
                if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best_score = float(sc)
                    best_p = p.copy()
                    print(f"downsampled h1 len={target} score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)

    # Portfolio over lengths.  Small lengths are explored most aggressively.
    total_budget = max(1.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    plan = [
        (2, 0.008, 8),
        (3, 0.018, 10),
        (4, 0.035, 12),
        (5, 0.105, 22),
        (6, 0.080, 18),
        (7, 0.060, 14),
        (8, 0.060, 12),
        (9, 0.050, 10),
        (10, 0.055, 10),
        (12, 0.055, 8),
        (14, 0.050, 7),
        (16, 0.060, 8),
        (20, 0.045, 5),
        (24, 0.035, 4),
        (32, 0.030, 3),
        (48, 0.025, 2),
        (64, 0.025, 2),
        (96, 0.020, 1),
        (128, 0.012, 1),
    ]

    for n, frac, planned_starts in plan:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        alloc = max(1.0, total_budget * float(frac))
        n_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + alloc)
        starts = max(1, int(planned_starts))
        if alloc / float(starts) < 0.25:
            starts = max(1, int(alloc / 0.35))

        for sid in range(starts):
            if time.monotonic() >= n_deadline:
                break

            start_deadline = min(n_deadline, time.monotonic() + max(0.25, alloc / float(starts)))

            p = _make_start(n, rng, sid + n)
            sc = _internal_score(p)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = float(sc)
                best_p = p.copy()
                print(f"n={n} start={sid} score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)

            if time.monotonic() >= start_deadline:
                continue

            if _HAVE_SCIPY and n <= 64:
                p2, sc2 = _lbfgs_refine(p, start_deadline)
            else:
                p2, sc2 = _mirror_refine(p, start_deadline, rng)

            sc = _internal_score(p2)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = float(sc)
                best_p = p2.copy()
                print(f"n={n} refined start={sid} score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)

        print(f"finished length {n}, current best={best_score:.10f} len={best_p.size}", flush=True)

    # Final concentrated polishing on the best sequence, especially if small.
    if best_p.size <= 512 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        finish = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + max(1.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.35))
        p2, sc2 = _local_top_gradient(best_p, finish, rng)
        sc = _internal_score(p2)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc)
            best_p = p2.copy()
            print(f"final local polish score={best_score:.10f} len={best_p.size}", flush=True)

    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # A few random restarts from the best sequence can escape final locals.
        nbest = int(best_p.size)
        for extra in range(12):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            sub_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + max(0.5, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.02))
            p0 = best_p.copy()
            z = np.log(p0 + 1e-300)
            z = z + 0.5 * rng.normal(size=nbest)
            z -= float(np.mean(z))
            z = np.clip(z, -8.0, 8.0)
            p = _softmax(z)
            p = p / float(p.sum())

            if _HAVE_SCIPY and nbest <= 64:
                p2, sc2 = _lbfgs_refine(p, sub_deadline)
            else:
                p2, sc2 = _mirror_refine(p, sub_deadline, rng)

            sc = _internal_score(p2)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = float(sc)
                best_p = p2.copy()
                print(f"extra restart {extra} score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)

    out = np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if out.size == 0:
        out = np.ones(4, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        out = np.ones(4, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(out.sum())

    scale = 100.0 / s
    mx = float(np.max(out))
    if mx > 0.0:
        scale = min(scale, 1000.0 / mx)
    out = out * max(0.0, scale)

    final_score = _internal_score(out)
    print(f"FINAL returned len={out.size} score={final_score:.10f}", flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
